Corrected 20 September 2026: the unconditional verbatim application to functional PHP is false for two-sided trees with no empty-hole branch; the full point-axiom argument and independently valid empty-branch applications remain. | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#switched-parent-old-image) |
| `cor:literal-term-readers-polylog-images` | With ell ~ n^{1/4} r^{-1/4} and s = ceil(log2(2M)), a common restriction gives all M readers of width-r literal terms images with certificates through r + 2 log2(2M), on a residual of Theta(n^{1/4} r^{-1/4}) points | Parameter corollary in the nonbipartite setting; bipartite constants unverified; readers with a parity input not covered | [Parameters and scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#switched-reader-parameters) |
| `lem:bipartite-matching-switching` | On the (n+1) x n board, for an r-disjunction and 1 <= s <= ell <= n-1, the fraction of size-(n-ell) partial matchings whose canonical matching decision tree has height >= s is at most (ell+1)^s sum_{j>=s/2} A^j <= 2((ell+1) sqrt(A))^s, A = 4 r ell (ell+1)/((n-ell+1) ln 2) <= 1/2 | Working proof adapted from Beame's q-matching argument with an explicit encoding (code, masks, partner indices in known vertex sets); encoding verified by exact round trips on small boards; constants generous; scoped correction 20 September 2026: the linked functional-image corollary has an unproved empty-hole/partition bridge. The counting statement and encoding are not refuted by that correction. | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-setting), [lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-lemma), [parameters and evidence](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-switching-parameters) |
| `lem:bit-term-decoder-image` | A conjunction of r bit literals on rho rows decodes, modulo I_r(F^fun), to the sum of the at most n^rho pairwise incompatible unary terms assigning each row a consistent hole | Working proof by row-wise expansion with functional exclusions | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bit-term-decoder-image) |
| `lem:sum-input-switched-image` | With inputs that are sums of pairwise incompatible unary terms and a representing, refining tree of height s, full leaf terms as coefficient images give companions and the coefficient identity through r+s and Booleanity through 2s | Working extension of `lem:switched-reader-old-image`; cross terms vanish by row conflicts | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#sum-input-switched-image) |
| `obs:restriction-family-obstruction` | The switching lemma's extension count needs uniform partial matchings, while the weight step needs the compact subcube family with a fixed residual subcube; in that family extensions give ratio C(N,j)(N+1)_j >= 1, and after a uniform restriction the residual is not affine; unary weight fails by ambient dimension mn | Decision from reading; sharpened by `lem:subcube-bit-switching` (light rows switch in the family) and `ex:fixed-subcube-pinned-rows-refuted` (pinned rows do not); the width-one subcube case is the recorded positive-literal swap count | [Obstruction and openings](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#restriction-family-obstruction) |
| `lem:subcube-bit-switching` | In the slack subcube family Phi_e (N+1+e residual pigeons, e free outside labels, fixed residual subcube of size N), a reader with <= r rows per term and <= w literals per row, w <= l-l_2-1, e >= 2^{w+2} s, has canonical-tree height >= s with probability <= A^s + M r exp(-e 2^{-w-4}), A = 2r(N+e)e(N+e+1)/(n-N-e+1); the tree transfers to the compact family Phi_0; with e=N, N^3 <= n/(48r), w <= log2 N - log2 s - 6, s = ceil(log2(8M)) one restriction serves all readers | Working proof by an extension encoding whose code stays in Phi_{e-s}; encoding verified by 76,418 exact round trips at n in {8,16}; composes with `lem:bit-term-decoder-image`, `lem:sum-input-switched-image`, and the weight step for rows of at most about (1/3) log2 n literals | [Lemma, proof, parameters](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-lemma) |
| `ex:fixed-subcube-pinned-rows-refuted` | For the fixed subcube Q, the reader OR_i [row i pinned to q_{i mod N}] (n+1 one-row terms of l literals, rank l <= r_high) has, for all but an N^{-1/10} fraction of Phi_0, no matching decision tree of height below N/(2 log2 N + 35) whose leaves falsify every term or contain a satisfied term | Refutation by an adversary argument, all sizes; concerns syntactic representation, not representation modulo the residual ideal | [Claim and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-refutation-fixed) |
| `obs:subcube-code-obstruction` | An extension encoding in any family whose residual holes form a flat gains only from the residual pigeons and can record a residual row only by matching it to a consistent free label outside the flat, so it covers exactly rows with at most about log2(e/s) literals; a uniformly random flat defeats the pinned-row reader and keeps the lemma, leaving row widths between (1/3) log2 n and (c-1/3) log2 n open for M = n^c | Obstruction with a proposed family; the affine invariance of the weight step under a random flat is not verified | [Sections 4 and 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#subcube-switching-code-obstruction) |
| `lem:random-flat-restriction-preservation` | Restricting to a uniformly random affine flat Q of dimension l_2 (live rows uniform, others matched bijectively outside Q) maps degree-d old-bit polynomials to degree <= d in the residual bits, maps Q_n into the ideal of Q_N with certificates through l, and keeps the affine decoder consistent modulo the residual row axiom; the multiplier space, flat count, and cube detector apply verbatim on Q | Working proof; the recorded coordinate restriction is the special case of a coordinate subcube | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#random-flat-preservation) |
| `thm:random-flat-literal-switching` | In the random-flat family, a reader whose light part (rows below w_0 literals) satisfies the subcube switching hypotheses has, with probability >= 1 - A^s - M r exp(-e 2^{-w-4}) - M_h r (N+1+e)(N+2r) 2^{-w_0}/n, a tree of height < s whose 1-leaves contain a satisfied term and whose 0-leaves falsify every light term while heavy terms are falsified or vanish modulo residual Booleanity; hence complete switching for sources of size S <= n/(768 s r (N+2r)); refined on 2026-09-18 (`entry-2026-09-18-literal-window`) to (N+1) in place of (N+1+e), giving completeness up to n^{4/3}/polylog and a window from (1/3) log2 n to (c-1) log2 n for S = n^c, c > 4/3 | Working proof (heavy-term dichotomy, no change to the canonical tree; light lemma round-trip tested on random flats with 54,713 exact round trips); the window is the remaining literal-frontier gap | [Theorem, proof, parameters](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#random-flat-heavy-dichotomy) |
| `ex:q-skip-tree-encoding-refuted` | Making the canonical tree skip terms with a row that has no consistent unused hole of Q breaks the extension encoding: a moved row's consistent outside code label can revive a skipped term for the decoder | Refutation by round-trip failures (114 to 1,206 per run) with the mechanism read off a failing case; does not apply to the wide-round tree, whose skip tests only unplaced wide rows (`lem:wide-annihilation`, 2026-09-18) | [Section 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#q-skip-refuted) |
| `lem:hole-query-node-functional` | A hole-query node with branches X_{jq} over uncovered pigeons and the empty branch u_q = 1 - sum_j X_{jq} has branch factors summing to one identically, pairwise products zero modulo Boolean and exclusion axioms at degree two, and idempotent u_q; trees with such nodes keep leaf degree <= height, the leaf partition identity, and pairwise incompatibility, so the switched-reader image lemmas extend verbatim without any onto axiom | Working proof | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-query-nodes) |
| `obs:literal-window-structure` | The light range (1/3) log2 n is intrinsic to extension encodings in flat families (the free-set entropy is the gain; a fixed free pool only raises the family ratio); a window term is alive-unsatisfied with a Q-consistent heavy row with probability <= r 4^r (N+1)N/n times the product of its pattern densities, against >= 2^{-2r-1} times the product for satisfaction, so the window matters only for readers with correlated satisfaction events; the sufficient obligation is a bound on >= k_0 alive window terms with distinct heavy rows and no satisfied term (corrected on 2026-09-18 by `ex:random-flat-pigeon-only-refuted`: the holes must also be distinct, and the tree must use hole queries) | Observation with the refuted fixed-pool variant and a named sufficient obligation; scope narrowed 2026-09-18: the (1/3) log2 n limit holds for encodings that move any residual row of the slack tree in Phi_e (the residual factor N+e+1), while the pair-space encoding of the restricted tree moves rows of R' only, keeps the free-set entropy, and admits e up to n^(2-eps-c)/4400 (`lem:pair-space-decoder`, [correction of scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-encoding)); the fixed-pool refutation stands; `obs:shared-wide-cube` (2026-09-18) shows hole queries necessary per reader already at size n for one wide pattern shared by n/2 rows | [Section 3](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#window-structure) |
| `ex:random-flat-pigeon-only-refuted` | In the random-flat family the single-target reader F_{x*} = OR_i [row i pinned to x*] is, with probability N/n (the event x* in Q), unsatisfied and without any pigeon-only deciding tree of height < N-1, while one hole query at x* decides it; the n readers F_{x*} (size n^2) contain a bad one for every restriction | Refutation, working proof; corrects the window obligation to distinct holes; corrected again on 2026-09-18 (`obs:alive-pair-matching-obligation`) to a matching bound, with the rank effect (N/n)^{rank} for prescribed labels in a random flat | [Claim and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pigeon-only-random-flat-refutation) |
| `obs:alive-pair-matching-obligation` | For unary heavy rows the alive pinned pairs (residual row, label in Q) form a bipartite graph B; a tree decides the pinned part in height equal to the vertex cover number = matching number nu(B) (König), and no tree does better when no term is satisfied; the readers OR_x [i_0 -> x] and F_{x*} show that distinct holes alone or distinct rows alone do not suffice; dense pinned classes (>= n ln(8SK) pairs) are satisfied by matched edges when their tail is (negative association of a uniform bijection, imported from Joag-Dev and Proschan 1983), sparse classes have few alive pairs in expectation; the missing step is an exponential tail for nu(B) over the random flat | Observation and reduction principle with an imported lemma; the general bound is open | [Sections 1 and 2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#alive-pair-graph) |
| `lem:dense-labels-satisfied` | Given (Q, R'), if at least Delta_0 = N + 2(n-N) ln(8S)/D* labels have degree >= D* >= 2(N+1) in a unary reader, some term is satisfied by the uniform bijection mu' with probability >= 1 - 1/(8S) | Conditional at the original threshold: the cited permutation negative-association step is false; a separately indexed larger threshold now has a valid permanent-based proof. | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dense-labels-satisfied) |
| `lem:sparse-matching-tail` | Alive pinned pairs with labels of degree < D* = k_1(n-k_1)/(2e(N+1)N) have matching number >= k_1 with probability <= 2^{-k_1}, by the symmetric sum of degrees over the flat's N points and the uniform residual set | Working proof; uses only \|Q\| = N, no affine structure; bounds the heavy rounds of the both-endpoints tree directly ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#sparse-matching-tail) |
| `lem:dense-labels-in-flat` | A set D of labels meets a uniformly random flat of size N in >= k_2 points with probability <= \|D\|^{k_2} (N/n)^{ceil(log2 k_2)+1} | Working proof by affine rank and transitivity | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dense-labels-in-flat) |
| `thm:unary-reader-hole-query-switching` | In the random-flat family a unary reader has, with probability >= 1 - 1/(8S) - Delta_0^{k_2}(N/n)^{ceil(log2 k_2)+1} - 2^{-k_1}, a pigeon-and-hole-query tree of height <= k_1 + k_2 whose 1-leaves contain a satisfied term and whose 0-leaves falsify or annihilate every term; for S = n^c readers, k_1 = ceil(log2(16S)), k_2 = 2^{c+2}, one restriction serves all unary readers with height O(log S + 2^c); readers with separate light and unary terms are covered with the light encoding | Working proof; first literal-frontier result without a size regime; mixed terms (pinned row with light tail) open; corrected 2026-09-18: the separate-terms corollary must run the light part first, since a pinned row that is light elsewhere is invisible to the decoder ([two-role rows](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-rows)) Unary-only repair now proved separately with Delta_B; this combined original record remains conditional at its original constants and composition scope. | [Theorem and corollaries](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#unary-switching-theorem) |
| `lem:adaptive-heavy-query-rule` | In the canonical tree for mixed readers, querying the pigeon when its row has at least two unkilled pairs of B_0 (residual row, label in Q, tails ignored) and otherwise the hole makes every path's heavy queries pairwise distinct in rows and holes, hence at most nu(B_t) with B_t the pairs whose tail is not falsified; the decoder recomputes the rule from the code, so the light encoding applies with heavy queries recorded unmoved | Working proof; the probabilistic bound on nu(B_t) for mixed readers is decomposed into satisfied, alive-matched, and alive-unmatched classes and left open; part (ii) corrected 2026-09-18: a light query at a row pinned in an earlier heavy round is recorded, not moved, costing one factor N+e per heavy query ([recording rule](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-rows)); corrected again 2026-09-18: the recording rule may cost every light query for two-role rows, so part (ii) holds for role-separated readers ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-gap)); part (i) refuted 2026-09-18: pigeon queries can repeat a trigger hole, so the distinct-holes and nu(B_t) claims fail; replaced by the hole-only rule ([counterexample and correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap)); the conclusion of part (i) holds for the both-endpoints rule ([rule](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)) | [Lemma and decomposition](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-heavy-query-rule) |
| `lem:matched-first-preference` | Preferring, among non-falsified terms, the earliest whose pinned pair is matched makes every heavy query come from a class with no matched pinned pair, so the heavy queries of a path number at most nu(B_um), the matching number of alive pairs of unmatched classes with non-falsified tails; decoder-computable | Working proof; the reader OR_x OR_i [i -> x] and [j in p_x] is shown not to be a counterexample because classes with labels outside Q are matched by the occupants, leaving a rank bound for survivor sets as the last step; corrected 2026-09-18: on the hole-only tree the bound is the hole count of those pairs, not the matching number ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-rule)); restored as stated on the both-endpoints tree ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)) | [Lemma and analysis](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#matched-first-preference) |
| `obs:window-height-budget` | The residual weight step needs image degrees 2s+r <= N/4, so tree height N/8-r suffices for the window and N is a free polylogarithmic parameter; heavy queries number at most 2N+1 on any path, so every literal reader switches at every polynomial size with height 6N+3c, and the open mixed-term obligation is a constant-factor bound (at most N/24 heavy queries on paths with few light answers) | Working observation; enumerating light answers cannot prove the constant-factor bound (union n^(k+c) against rank gain (N^2/n)^(log k)); corrected 2026-09-18 ([two-role correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-gap): switching conclusion for role-separated readers; the heavy bound stands); on the hole-only tree the trivial count is N ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-rule)); on the both-endpoints tree: N rounds trivially, obligation fewer than N/48 rounds ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)); the two-role restriction is lifted for sparsely pinned readers by `lem:two-role-decoder` on the complete-term tree (2026-09-18); the composition sketch and the trivial O(N) height for mixed readers are unproved on the slack family (`obs:composition-gap`, 2026-09-18) Correction 2026-09-20: the inference that 2t+r<=N/4 alone suffices for the residual weight step is withdrawn; multiplier k>=2, low-map fit and the full proof ceiling are additional requirements. Switching height/probability statements have their separate scopes. | [Budget, trivial count, sharpened obligation](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#window-height-budget) |
| `lem:alive-unmatched-comparison` | For any pair set P in the random-flat family, Pr[some pair alive and none matched] <= 45(N+1)^2 ln(n)/n * Pr[some pair matched]; no structure of P or of the flat is used | Working proof (inclusion-exclusion lower bound, negative association upper bound, heavy rows and labels); sharp up to the logarithm | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#alive-unmatched-comparison) |
| `lem:tail-comparison` | A light tail on at most r-1 rows is alive but not satisfied by the restriction with probability at most 2(r-1)2^w(N+1)/n times the probability that it is satisfied, also conditionally on the other rows and the flat | Working proof; with the light range this factor is at most rN n^(-2/3) | [Lemma and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#tail-comparison) |
| `cor:eligibility-expectation` | For any mixed reader, the expected number of classes eligible to trigger a heavy query is at most theta'(1+eps_w) times the expected number of classes with a satisfied term, theta' = 45(N+1)^2 ln(n)/n (1+o(1)) | Working corollary in expectation; the bad-event bound needs a correlation argument by rows, since exposing all tail rows determines the flat | [Corollary and obstacle](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#eligibility-corollary) |
| `thm:single-row-full-class-switching` | Mixed readers whose classes are full (all rows pinned to one label) with single-row light tails on pairwise distinct rows have, at every polynomial source size n^c, at most 2^(2c+2) heavy queries on every path except with probability n^(-c-1)+n^(-2c-2): many labels force a satisfied term by negative association over distinct rows, few labels bound the trigger holes by the affine rank of the label set in the flat | Working theorem with full proof (random-flat family, hole-query trees); shared tail rows and non-full pair sets excluded and their required mechanisms named; corrected 2026-09-18 ([two-role correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-gap): switching conclusion for role-separated readers; the heavy bound stands); holds on the hole-only tree, whose trigger holes are distinct; false for the adaptive tree when c >= 2 ([correction 2026-09-18](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap)); on the both-endpoints tree the count bounds heavy rounds, with twice as many queries ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)); unary readers are contained in `thm:single-row-wide-readers` on the wide-round tree (2026-09-18) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#single-row-full-theorem), [limits](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#single-row-full-limits) |
| `thm:bounded-loading-switching` | Single-row full-class readers on arbitrary rows with loading Lambda(F) = sum over rows of the fraction of labels consistent with a class at most lambda_0 have fewer than (2c+2+max(4e lambda_0,(c+1)log n))2^(c+3) heavy queries on every path except with probability 3n^(-c-1): few hosting rows are residual, few matched rows are loaded (negative association), and each assigned row's survivor set in the flat is bounded by affine rank | Working theorem with full proof; needs no satisfied term; does not imply the distinct-rows theorem; corrected 2026-09-18 ([two-role correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-gap): switching conclusion for role-separated readers; the heavy bound stands); holds on the hole-only tree, whose trigger holes are distinct; false for the adaptive tree when c >= 2 ([correction 2026-09-18](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap)); on the both-endpoints tree the count bounds heavy rounds, with twice as many queries ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)) Scoped replacement now recorded separately: Repairs only the positive row-hit estimate in step (ii) for integer k<= (n+1)/2; no full round or switching claim. | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bounded-loading-theorem) |
| `lem:out-loading-satisfaction` | Given the flat, the probability that no term is satisfied is at most exp(-sum over rows of the number of labels outside the flat consistent with a class of that row whose label is outside the flat, divided by n+1) | Working proof by negative association over rows; a bad reader's loading outside the flat is at most (c+1) ln n Scoped replacement now recorded separately: Replaces the exponent constant one by log(2); original constant remains conditional. | [Lemma and residual case](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#out-loading-lemma) |
| `obs:full-class-holes-are-labels` | For full-class readers trigger holes lie in the reader label set inside Q; on the both-endpoints tree they are distinct rounds. For at most n^(2/3) labels, the corrected threshold is 2^(3c+4) rounds with failure at most n^(-c-1) in the stated asymptotic regime. | Working corrected reading under the historical rank-step correction: larger threshold 2^(3c+4), with round counting on the both-endpoints tree and at most twice as many heavy queries. The old adaptive-tree and smaller-threshold statements are not reinstated. [Correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction) | [Observation and residual case](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#label-set-observation) |
| `thm:single-row-full-class-window` | Every mixed reader whose classes are full (all rows pinned to one label) with a single-row light tail, on arbitrary rows, has fewer than N/24 heavy queries on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1), for N at least 2^(c+11)(c+2)^2 ln^2 n; six exclusions organized by labels (heavy labels in the flat and few, large survivor sets outside the flat, light out-loading forces a satisfied term, the flat's light loading is small by charging light-good pairs to light labels, few matched and few residual rows yield light holes) | Working theorem with full proof; settles the window obligation for this class at every polynomial size within the height budget; contains the distinct-rows, bounded-loading, and label-set results; reviewed 2026-09-18: self pairs excluded from the light loading, threshold N_0 = 2^(2c+24)(c+2) ln n, assigned rows are those assigned before the last heavy query ([review](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#entry-2026-09-18-srf-review)); corrected 2026-09-18 ([two-role correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-gap): switching conclusion for role-separated readers; the heavy bound stands); holds on the hole-only tree, whose trigger holes are distinct; false for the adaptive tree when c >= 2 ([correction 2026-09-18](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap)); on the both-endpoints tree: fewer than N/48 rounds, statement unchanged ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands The combined original remains conditional; new round proof repairs constants, conditioning and nonempty charging but does not reinstate its decoder. | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#srf-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#srf-scope) |
| `ex:mixed-tree-round-trip` | The mixed canonical tree (matched-first preference, adaptive heavy rule, hole queries) and its encoding decode correctly on every rich bad path of six exhaustively enumerated role-separated instances (n=8, N=2, 3147 paths), and a constructed reader with a row pinned in one term and light in another fails on 2610 of 4050 and 2130 of 4450 paths | Finite check with positive and negative controls; tool research/tools/check_mixed_term_encoding.py; extended 2026-09-18 with the hole-only rule: 3263 rich bad paths, 0 failures, two-role example still fails ([results](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-tool)); both-endpoints rule 2026-09-18: 15219 rich bad paths, 0 failures, two-role example fails on 1800 of 6380 ([results](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-tool)); complete-term rule 2026-09-18: two-role example 6640 rich bad paths 0 failures, random two-role readers 0 failures ([runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-tool)) | [Results](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#mixed-tool-results) |
| `ex:adaptive-rule-pigeon-counterexample` | A full-column reader with k >= 2 classes labelled in the flat, tails satisfied, and every residual row pinned makes the adaptive tree take N-k+1 pigeon queries on one path, all triggered by the same hole, while nu(B_t) = k; for k = 2 the configuration has probability of order N^2/n^2, so the adaptive-tree heavy bounds fail for c >= 2 | Refutation, verified by computation (n = 16, N = 8, three classes: adaptive tree 8 heavy queries, hole-only tree 3); tool research/tools/check_adaptive_rule_counterexample.py; the wide-round tree avoids the adaptive choice by a cover fixed at the root (`lem:wide-cover-bound`, 2026-09-18) | [Gap and counterexample](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#adaptive-rule-gap), [tool records](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-tool) |
| `lem:hole-only-rule` | Querying the trigger hole at every heavy round (branches over uncovered residual rows and the empty branch) makes the trigger holes of a path pairwise distinct, so heavy queries number at most the hole count of the alive non-falsified pinned pairs, at most N, each answer among N+2 values; for role-separated readers the light encoding applies with the heavy answers recorded and nothing to recompute | Working proof; replaces the adaptive rule in every trigger-hole theorem; decoder verified by exhaustive round trips (3263 role-separated rich bad paths, 0 failures); insufficient for the general window 2026-09-18: a pinned row carrying many labels gives N heavy queries ([failure](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-sparse-failure)); superseded by the both-endpoints rule, valid for the full-column theorems | [Rule, lemma, affected claims](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-rule) |
| `lem:light-row-matching-satisfaction` | In a bipartite full-column reader, if the graph of matched light rows against hosted light labels outside the flat has a matching of size 12*2^w(c+1) ln n, some term is satisfied except with probability n^(-c-1): the events "row j consistent with its matched label x_j and x_j occupied by a pinned row" use disjoint rows and columns, so negative association gives the product | Working proof; the cover alternative (Konig) bounds the loading in the residual case | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#matching-lemma) |
| `thm:bipartite-full-column-switching` | Bipartite full-column readers (pinned rows A, light rows B with at most (n-2N-2)/2 rows, classes A x {x} with a single-row light tail on B) have fewer than N/24 heavy queries on every path of the hole-only tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1) for N at least 2^(2c+24)(c+3)^2 ln^2 n, and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget on that tree, whose decoder is verified | Working theorem with full proof: heavy labels dormant or satisfied (negative association over columns and rows), survivor sets small (dormancy or rank), matching or cover, the flat's small light loading, matched and residual light rows; first mixed class with both the heavy and the light bound; holds on the both-endpoints tree with fewer than N/48 rounds ([transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule)); contained in the two-tier theorem; corrected 2026-09-18: step (F4) gives \|X_theta cap Q\| < 2^(2c+2), threshold N_0 = 2^(3c+24)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n, statement otherwise unchanged ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction)); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#bipartite-scope) |
| `ex:hole-only-sparse-failure` | The unary reader OR_x [i_0 -> x] with i_0 residual (probability (N+1)/(n+1)) has a path of N heavy queries in the hole-only tree, one pigeon query in the adaptive tree, and one round in the both-endpoints tree | Refutation of the hole-only rule for general readers, verified by computation (n = 16, N = 8: 8, 1, 2 heavy queries); the full-column theorems on the hole-only tree stand | [Failure](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#hole-only-sparse-failure), [tool records](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-tool) |
| `lem:both-endpoints-rule` | Querying the trigger pigeon and then, unless it took the trigger hole, the trigger hole makes the trigger pairs of a path pairwise disjoint, a matching in B_t; rounds at most nu(B_t) <= N, queries at most twice that, answers among (N+e)(N+2) values per round; for role-separated readers the light encoding applies with the answers recorded | Working proof; every recorded heavy-query bound becomes a bound on rounds (the N/24 theorems keep their statements: fewer than N/48 rounds); decoder verified by exhaustive round trips (15219 role-separated rich bad paths, 0 failures); the complete-term rule (`lem:complete-term-rule`, 2026-09-18) reorders the round before the tail and keeps the matching | [Rule, lemma, transfer](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#both-endpoints-rule) |
| `lem:two-tier-matching-satisfaction` | In a column reader, if the graph of matched light rows against light labels outside the flat hosted by dense classes (at least n/4 pinned rows) has a matching of size 20*2^w(c+1) ln n, some term is satisfied except with probability n^(-c-1) | Working proof by negative association over disjoint rows and columns; Konig gives the cover otherwise | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-lemma) |
| `thm:two-tier-column-switching` | Column readers (pinned rows A, light rows B, classes I x {x} with I in A and a single-row light tail on B) whose classes pin at least n/4 rows or are sparse with the sparse classes at each label pinning fewer than n/(400e(N+1)) rows in total have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 8n^(-c-1) for N at least 2^(2c+29)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n, and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget | Working theorem with full proof: sparse rounds by the sparse lemma, dense rounds by the full-column steps with the occupant outside the class's rows in place of dormancy (heavy labels via rows where the dense loading is large, survivor sets, matching or cover, flat loading, matched and residual rows); contains the bipartite full-column theorem; the middle tier (labels whose sparse classes pin many rows) is the open case; corrected 2026-09-18: step (G4) gives 2^(2c+2), threshold N_1 = 2^(3c+28)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction)); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-tier-scope) |
| `lem:activated-sparse-matching-tail` | If for every label x and every light assignment the activated degree (union over the activated classes at each tail row, summed over tail rows) is below D* = n/(400e(N+1)), then the alive pairs with non-falsified tails have matching number >= N/192 with probability <= 2^(-N/192): the tail events are increasing on distinct rows, so negative association bounds them by a product measure on the light rows, and the symmetric sum over N points closes as in the sparse lemma | Working proof; contained up to the constant in the activated union lemma, which conditions on the light rows and takes a union across tail rows ([lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activated-union-lemma)) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activated-sparse-lemma) |
| `thm:activation-bounded-column-switching` | Column readers whose every label is two-tier (sparse classes pin fewer than D* rows) or activation-bounded (activated sparse degree below D* for every light assignment) satisfy the two-tier theorem's conclusion with the same constants | Working proof; extends the two-tier theorem (labels with sparse classes on few tail rows or with pairwise inconsistent patterns); labels whose sparse classes sit on many tail rows with compatible patterns remain; threshold corrected with the two-tier theorem ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction)); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activation-bounded-theorem) |
| `lem:two-stage-exposure-reduction` | For an arbitrary column reader, after exposing the flat, the residual pinned rows and the pinned rows' labels, the probability of a path with k rounds and no 1-leaf is at most the sum over k-matchings of alive pairs and class choices of the product over tail rows of the unblocked activation weights (labels consistent with the chosen patterns, in the remaining labels, activating no matched class at that row, plus the residual probability) | Working proof by negative association of the light bijection; the general column claim reduces to integrating this count over the exposure | [Reduction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-stage-reduction) |
| `conj:general-column-claim` | Every column reader (classes I x {x}, I in the pinned rows, single-row light tails, role-separated) of a source of size n^c has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability O(n^(-c-1)) | Conjecture; proved for two-tier or activation-bounded labels; three reader families (private labels, shared labels, one hosting row) fail to refute it: rounds need activated edge mass of order n^2/N or a rank coincidence, and that mass forces a satisfied term; proved 2026-09-18 for row-spread readers (each light row hosts fewer than n^(1/3) labels); the remaining case is a row hosting many labels with many patterns ([theorem](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-theorem), [remaining case](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-scope)); settled 2026-09-18 for all widths w <= l/(2 log_2(2l+1)) and for any reader with at most H_max patterns per light row ([theorem](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pattern-bounded-theorem)); open only for wide patterns crowded on one light row; for tails on any rows it holds at every size n^c, c < 2-eps, by `cor:multirow-size-switching`, and above that size under the light-mass hypothesis of `thm:multirow-mass-switching`, and with at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows at every size by `thm:few-light-rows-switching`, and for sizes n^c, c < 3-2eps, with tails on boundedly many rows by `cor:crowded-size-switching` | [Claim](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#general-column-claim), [attempts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#column-falsification) |
| `lem:activated-union-degree-tail` | Given the flat and the light rows, if every label's activated union of pinned rows (over the classes whose tail row is residual or carries a consistent label) has fewer than n/(800e(N+1)) rows, the alive activated pairs have matching number >= N/192 with probability <= 2^(-N/192): the residual pinned rows form a uniform set of size at most N+1, and the symmetric sum over N points closes | Working proof; contains the sparse lemma and, up to the constant, the product-measure lemma | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#activated-union-lemma) |
| `thm:row-spread-column-switching` | Column readers whose light rows each host classes of fewer than n^(1/3) distinct labels have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-1), for N >= 2^(c+11)(c+1) log n and 2^w <= n^(1/3)/(2^15 e(N+1)(c+1) ln n), and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget | Working theorem with full proof and no density or loading hypothesis: labels with a large activated union are few (rank) or many (their occupants match many classes on many rows, satisfied by the light bijection); incomparable with the full-column and two-tier theorems; corrected 2026-09-18: the few-middle-labels case needs 2^(3c+4) and N >= 2^(3c+10)(c+1) log n ([correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction)); contained in the pattern-bounded theorem; switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#row-spread-scope) |
| `thm:pattern-bounded-switching` | Role-separated readers with single-row light tails and any pinned pairs whose light rows carry at most H_max = n^(2/3)/(3300eN(N+1)J_0) patterns, J_0 = 32*2^w(c+1) ln n + N + 1, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 3n^(-c-1), for N >= 2^(3c+10)(c+1) log n and 2^w <= n^(1/3), and switch at every polynomial size within the height budget; in particular every such reader of width w <= l/(2 log_2(2l+1)) | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (fresh-context review, dependencies re-read, recorded counterexamples checked): heavy hosts (mass >= 2N(n+1)) have a matched pair and block their row; light labels by the activated union lemma; labels with a large light union are few (rank with 2^(3c+4)) or carry mass on many rows, whose matched pairs are satisfied by the light bijection; contains the role-separated column theorems; contained, with H_max halved, in `thm:multirow-mass-switching` (2026-09-18, [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-scope)); the crowded single-row case it left open is closed for readers with at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows by `thm:few-light-rows-switching` (2026-09-18); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pattern-bounded-theorem), [hosts](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#host-setting), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pattern-bounded-scope) |
| `cor:rank-step-correction` | The independent-points rank bound (\|X\|N/n)^(d+1) is below n^(-c-1) for \|X\| <= n^(2/3) only when d >= 3c+3, and for \|X\| <= n^(1/3) polylog when d >= 2c+2; four recorded steps used d = c+3 with such sets | Correction 2026-09-18 with statements kept under larger constants: label-set corollary (2^(3c+4)), bipartite (F4) and two-tier (G4) (2^(2c+2), thresholds 2^(3c+24)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n and 2^(3c+28)(c+4)^2 ln^2 n), row-spread few-middle-labels case (2^(3c+4), N >= 2^(3c+10)(c+1) log n); single-row full-class, bounded-loading, and distinct-rows theorems unaffected | [Correction](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#rank-step-correction) |
| `thm:multirow-mass-switching` | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails on any rows have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf, for N >= 2^(d_eps+6)(c+1) log2 n, d_eps = ceil((c+1)/eps)+1, if (i) at most n^(1-eps) labels have a light union of at least n/(800e(N+1)) pinned rows (probability 3n^(-c-1)), or (ii) tails have at most t rows and total width W and no light row carries light-host mass above M_eps = n^(2-eps)/(1600e(N+1)J_0), J_0 = 2t(2^(W+4t)(c+1) ln n + N + 1) (probability 4n^(-c-1)); they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded counterexamples checked, three-row-tail round trips 2255 rich bad paths 0 failures, fresh-context review with four findings resolved); part (ii) contains `thm:pattern-bounded-switching` with H_max halved up to the probability constant; hosts are tails, heavy hosts block their rows, a greedy row-disjoint family of J_0/t matched light hosts is satisfied by the light bijection; the rank step uses label sets of size n^(1-eps); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching clauses restored for role-separated readers under column-sparseness by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds in the pair-space light range with no further hypothesis (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-host-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-mass-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-scope) |
| `cor:multirow-size-switching` | Every role-separated reader of pinned rows with light tails of any shape and at most n^c terms, 1 <= c < 2-eps, 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c), has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path before a 1-leaf (probability 3n^(-c-1)) and switches within the height budget when its tails lie in the light lemma's range | Working corollary of part (i): a reader's light unions have total size at most its number of terms, so the mass step is never needed below size n^(2-eps); the first window statement beyond size n^(4/3) with no hypothesis on pinned pairs, labels, hosts, or tail shape; raised to size n^(3-2eps) for tails on boundedly many rows by `cor:crowded-size-switching` (2026-09-18); two-role form (sparse pinning, arbitrary tails, complete-term tree) by `cor:two-role-size-switching` (2026-09-18); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching for sources of total size below n^(2-eps) by `lem:dichotomy-switches-single-label-pins` (2026-09-18); the per-reader clause at c >= 1 stays conditional; switching clause restored for sparsely pinned readers with tails up to width (2-eps-c) log2 n - O(log N) by `thm:pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause restored with no pinning hypothesis for tails 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024, light range up to n/(2^9 r N^(5/2) s), by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds in the pair-space light range with no further hypothesis (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#multirow-size-corollary) |
| `thm:few-light-rows-switching` | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails of any shape on at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows, any pinned pairs and any size, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-2) for any c > 0, for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n with r_0 = ceil((c+2)/eps)+1, c' = c+2+r_0, d = ceil(c'/eps)+2; they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded counterexamples checked, standing reviewer); no heavy/light distinction and no mass, pattern, or width hypothesis: the light rows are exposed, at most r_0 are residual, a union bound over their answers fixes the consistent hosts, which are matched when their mass is large and otherwise have at most n^(1-eps) labels of large union; contains the crowded single light row named in `thm:pattern-bounded-switching` when the light rows are few; switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching clause restored under column-sparseness by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds in the pair-space light range with no further hypothesis (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#few-rows-scope) |
| `thm:crowded-rows-exposed-switching` | Role-separated readers of pinned rows with light tails on at most t rows (total width W, at most n^c terms) and at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 crowded light rows (light mass above M_eps under the heavy threshold 2(N + n^(1-eps)/N^4)(n+1)) have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the both-endpoints tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 6n^(-c-2), for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/N^4; they switch within the height budget when the tails lie in the light lemma's range | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded counterexamples checked, standing reviewer); combines `thm:multirow-mass-switching` and `thm:few-light-rows-switching`: the crowded rows are exposed, a union bound over the at most r_0 residual ones fixes the surviving hosts, which merge into a reduced reader on the uncrowded rows for steps (H1) to (H4); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-scope) |
| `cor:crowded-size-switching` | Every role-separated reader of size n^c with light tails on at most t rows of total width W and 1600e t (N+1) N^4 J_0 n^c <= n^(3-2eps), that is c < 3-2eps-o(1), has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path (probability 6n^(-c-2)) and switches within the height budget when its tails lie in the light lemma's range | Working corollary: the light mass totals at most t times the number of terms, so the crowded rows are at most t n^c/M_eps; raises the unconditional size bound of `cor:multirow-size-switching` from n^(2-eps) to n^(3-2eps); two-role form for few or light two-role rows by `cor:two-role-size-switching` (2026-09-18); switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching clause restored under column-sparseness by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds in the pair-space light range with no further hypothesis (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#crowded-size-corollary) |
| `lem:complete-term-rule` | On the complete-term tree (the both-endpoints round first, then every uncovered tail row of the current term whatever the round's outcome) the trigger pairs of a path form a matching in the alive pairs of hosts not falsified by rho' and without a matched pair, and every recorded round bound transfers, with fewer than r_0 rounds added for the theorems that expose light rows | Working proof; the added rounds are charged to exposed residual rows assigned at the round's node | [Rule and lemmas](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-rule) |
| `lem:two-role-decoder` | With code labels avoiding the moved row's pinned labels, the extension encoding of the complete-term tree decodes for every reader, two-role rows allowed; richness' holds with probability 1 - Mr exp(-e 2^(-w-5)) under sparse pinning (a row pinned to at most a quarter of any pattern cube on it) and e >= 2^(w+3) s | Working proof; verified by exhaustive round trips: the recorded two-role counterexample 6640 rich bad paths 0 failures (1800 of 6380 fail under the both-endpoints rule), two random two-role readers and two role-separated readers 0 failures; tool research/tools/check_mixed_term_encoding.py --rule complete; its device (complete-term rule, code labels outside the moved row's pinned labels) carries over to the compact composition (`thm:two-role-compact-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Decoder and richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-decoder), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-tool) |
| `thm:two-role-few-rows-switching` | Readers with at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows, two-role rows allowed, tails of any shape, any pinned pairs, any size, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the complete-term tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 4n^(-c-2), with N as in `thm:few-light-rows-switching`; under sparse pinning they switch within the height budget | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded counterexamples checked, tool run, standing reviewer); the first switching statement for two-role readers: rounds at two-role rows and rounds whose host the path falsifies are charged to the fewer than r_0 residual light rows; switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching clause restored under sparse pinning, column-sparseness and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 by `thm:two-role-compact-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds under sparse pinning and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 with no column-sparseness (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-few-rows-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#complete-term-scope) |
| `thm:two-role-exposed-switching` | Two-role readers (tails on at most t rows, at most n^c terms) on the complete-term tree, with heavy threshold 2(N + 2n^(1-eps)/N^4)(n+1), an exposed set of at most n^(1-eps)/N^4 light rows containing every crowded row, and light mass at most n^(2-eps)/(3200e(N+1)) on the two-role rows outside it, have fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability 6n^(-c-2), for N >= 2^(d+7) c' log2 n and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/N^4; under sparse pinning they switch within the height budget | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, standing reviewer); the combined theorem's proof with two-role rows in the exposed set treated as dormant mass and two-role rows outside it kept out of the greedy family at the cost of their light mass; switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; switching clause restored under sparse pinning, column-sparseness and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 by `thm:two-role-compact-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds under sparse pinning and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 with no column-sparseness (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Setting](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-setting), [theorem and proof](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-scope) |
| `cor:two-role-size-switching` | Every sparsely pinned two-role reader of size n^c, 1 <= c < 2-eps with 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c), whatever its tails, has fewer than N/48 heavy rounds on every path of the complete-term tree (probability 3n^(-c-1)) and switches within the height budget in the light lemma's range; with tails on at most t rows and J_0 <= n^(1-eps)/(2N^4), the same holds when 3200e t(N+1)N^4 J_0 n^c <= n^(3-2eps) provided its two-role rows number at most n^(1-eps)/(2N^4) or carry light mass at most n^(2-eps)/(3200e(N+1)) | Working corollaries: Corollary 1 through the role-independent steps (H1) to (H3) of `thm:multirow-mass-switching` and the transfer lemma `lem:complete-term-rule`, Corollary 2 through `thm:two-role-exposed-switching`; the two-role forms of `cor:multirow-size-switching` and `cor:crowded-size-switching`; switching clause conditional 2026-09-18: its composition with the light encoding is unproved (`obs:composition-gap`), the round bound stands; Corollary 1's switching holds for sources of total size below n^(2-eps) by `lem:dichotomy-switches-single-label-pins` (2026-09-18); both per-reader clauses stay conditional; switching clauses restored (Corollary 1 with no pinning hypothesis beyond sparse pinning for tails 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024) by `thm:two-role-compact-composition` (2026-09-18); switching clause holds under sparse pinning and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 with no column-sparseness (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Corollaries](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-many-corollary) |
| `obs:composition-gap` | The composition of a heavy-round bound with the light encoding into switching within the height budget, recorded only as the height-budget sketch, is unproved: the round bounds are proved for the compact family Phi_0, the extension encoding exists only on the slack family Phi_e, the recorded mixed-tree rule does not define the slack tree at a pin whose hole is a free outside label, the tool's rounds at such pins have matching number above N for c >= 2 - 2 theta, and the sketch's own arithmetic covers per-row width (2/7) log2 n, not (1/3) log2 n | Correction with obstruction (2026-09-18): every 'switches within the height budget' clause of the mixed-term theorems and the O(N) trivial height claim are conditional (the separate-terms corollary stands in its recorded order); every round bound stands; repair routes (R1) move pinned rows on the slack tree with a collision rule and a rank-recorded preference to test, (R4) narrow polylogarithmic tails re-proved on Phi_e, (R2) swap encoding on Phi_0 and (R3) direct slack-family bounds as obstacles; sources of total size below n^(2-eps) are switched by `lem:dichotomy-switches-single-label-pins`, and the composition is reduced on the pair space to a decoder for pins at free holes (marking cost open) and the satisfied-term steps at compact rows (2026-09-18); discharged for sparsely pinned role-separated readers by `thm:pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18); open for dense pinning (`obs:dense-pinning-obstruction`); discharged for role-separated readers by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18) through the swap-encoding route (the displaced row is the filling row of the code label, made visible by sigma; the own-row rule decides its pins): a composition on Phi_0 for the recorded tree with the recorded round bounds as input; open for two-role readers and dense pattern cubes (`obs:dense-cube-obstruction`); discharged for two-role readers under sparse pinning by `thm:two-role-compact-composition` (2026-09-18); discharged for every recorded round bound without column-sparseness (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Gap](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-statement), [status](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-status), [routes](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#composition-gap-routes) |
| `lem:dichotomy-switches-single-label-pins` | A reader of terms [i->x] & tail (tails of any width) or pin-free light terms with at most w literals per row in the light lemma's range, at most r rows per term, M terms of which M_h pinned, two-role rows and any pinning allowed, has under a uniform compact restriction a matching decision tree over Q of height below s whose 0-leaves falsify every pin-free term while every pinned term is falsified or vanishes, except with probability A^s + Mr exp(-e 2^(-w-4)) + M_h r (N+1)(N+2r)/n^2 (height 0 and the last term alone when every term is pinned); a source of total size n^c, c < 2, switches under one restriction with height O(log S) | Working lemma, by the proof of the heavy-term dichotomy with w_0 = ell, since a single-label pin is a full-width row; its per-reader failure n^(c-2) is tight, so it restores the switching of `cor:multirow-size-switching` and Corollary 1 of `cor:two-role-size-switching` for total size below n^(2-eps) only, and their per-reader clauses at size n^c, c >= 1, stay conditional on the composition; the per-reader composition it cannot give is `thm:pair-space-composition` for sparse pinning (2026-09-18) | [Lemma](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dichotomy-restores-lemma), [composition analysis](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#dichotomy-restores-composition) |
| `lem:pair-space-restriction` | Pair space with the filling on a fixed e-set S of rows outside every tail: rho uniform in Phi_e(S), rho'' a uniform bijection S -> E(rho); rho' = rho u rho'' is uniform in Phi_0(S), and Pr over Phi_0 of any event is at most (7/5) times its average over S of Pr over Phi_0(S) when e(N+1) <= n/8 (Lemma A0); the slack tree (complete-term tree on the slack instance, hole queried after every pigeon answer, pin test at a pin with a free hole) restricted by the filling is a valid matching decision tree for rho' whose height is the number of real queries (Lemma A) | Working proofs, passed through the verification gate (standing reviewer) | [Setting and lemmas](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-setting) |
| `lem:pair-space-decoder` | The encoding (rho*, sigma, beta, delta) of the restricted slack tree, rows of S recorded by nothing, code labels in E(rho) n C(p) \ X, is injective (Lemma B); the bad fraction is at most A''^s (2N(N+2))^nu_0 with A'' = 2rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1), the family ratio prod (N+1-i)(e-i)/(n-N-e+1+i) paying only for the N+1 compact residual rows (Lemma C); richness' needs sparse pinning, \|X n C(p)\| <= \|C(p)\|/4 | Working proofs; decoder verified by exhaustive round trips on n = 8, N = 4 boards (batch 4: no failure on or off G in nine runs; negative controls fail as predicted); tool research/tools/check_pair_space_encoding.py --encoding entry --fill-domain notail --pin-test --hole-always | [Encoding, decoder, count](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-encoding), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-tool) |
| `lem:pair-space-round-bound` | Steps (H1) to (H3) of `thm:multirow-mass-switching` hold on Phi_e(S) with S uniform in A: heavy threshold 2(N+2e)(n+1), D_2^(e) = n/(1600 e_Euler (N+1+e)), union bound (4(N+1+e)/n)^k; at most N/48 - 1 rounds on every path of the slack tree before a 1-leaf except with probability 3n^(-c-1) when the labels with a large light union number at most n^(1-eps), in particular for readers of size n^c when 1600 e_Euler (N+1+e) <= n^(2-eps-c) | Working proof following the multi-row theorem's part (i); unnecessary after `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` (2026-09-18), which uses the recorded round bounds on Phi_0 directly | [Lemma E](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-round-bound) |
| `thm:pair-space-composition` | Composition on the pair space: a sparsely pinned role-separated reader (at most n^c terms, \|X_L^(e)\| <= n^(1-eps) or 1600 e_Euler (N+1+e) <= n^(2-eps-c), tails with 2^(w+3) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), N >= 8(c+1) log2 n + (2r+1)(16 + 4 log2 N) and as in the multi-row theorem) admits under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 a matching decision tree over Q of height below N/8 - 2r with correct leaves, except with probability 7n^(-c-1); light range up to width (2-eps-c) log2 n - O(log N) through the size route | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded obstructions checked, tool run, standing reviewer); the composition that `obs:composition-gap` found missing, for sparse pinning; superseded 2026-09-18 by `thm:compact-pair-space-composition`, which needs no slack tree, no pin test and no transferred round bound, and no sparse pinning beyond column-sparseness | [Theorems D and F](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-round-bound) |
| `obs:dense-pinning-obstruction` | For readers whose pins cover more than a quarter of some tail pattern cube, the pair-space decoder cannot tell a pin at a free code label from a dead pin (the tool's first design fails off G on 895 of 4770 and similar); naming code labels or slots costs more than the family ratio gains; such readers, and two-role readers, have no composition; the satisfied-term and exposure steps are not transferred | Obstruction with recorded failing runs; narrowed 2026-09-18 by the own-row rule (`lem:own-row-richness`): dense pinning is an obstruction only for readers above size n^(5/3) whose pins fill a tail pattern cube (`obs:dense-cube-obstruction`); its two-role part closed under sparse pinning by `thm:two-role-compact-composition` (2026-09-18); only the dense-cube part remains; closed 2026-09-18 by `thm:current-term-composition` | [Section 4](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#pair-space-obstruction) |
| `lem:compact-pair-space-decoder` | The extension encoding of the compact complete-term tree T_c(F, rho') on the pair space (filling on an e-set S of rows outside every tail; code labels in E(rho) n C(p) whose own filling row is not pinned to them; sigma, positions, answers) is injective: the decoder simulates T_c from rho*, sigma and a mark for the rows filled at code labels, treating a pin at an outside label as satisfied exactly when the row's known label is that label; the bad fraction among rich pairs is at most A''^s (2N(N+2))^nu_0, A'' = 2rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1) | Working proofs (Lemmas B and C), passed through the verification gate; decoder verified by exhaustive round trips (batch 5: no failure on or off G in eleven runs on n = 8, N = 4 boards, all labels pinned in six of them; controls fail as predicted); tool research/tools/check_pair_space_encoding.py --tree compact --code-rule own; own-row rule replaced by the current-term rule in `lem:current-term-decoder` (2026-09-18) | [Code and decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-code), [runs](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-tool) |
| `lem:own-row-richness` | For a column-sparse reader (in every tail pattern cube at most a quarter of the labels of C(p) n O are pinned by more than \|A\|/64 rows) with w <= l - l_2 - 1 and 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/16, the pair fails to have s valid labels for some tail pattern with probability at most n 2^(-e/29) + 2Mr exp(-s/3); a reader with at most n^c terms is column-sparse whenever 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024 | Working proof (hypergeometric bounds and a sequential domination of the filling); unnecessary under the current-term rule (`thm:current-term-composition`, 2026-09-18) | [Lemma R](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-richness) |
| `thm:compact-pair-space-composition` | Composition on Phi_0 for the recorded tree: a column-sparse role-separated reader with tails 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)) whose compact complete-term tree has at most N/48 - 1 rounds on every path before a 1-leaf except with probability p_R switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + (7/5)(n 2^(-e/29) + 2Mr exp(-s/3) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+1)(2 + log2 N/2))), i.e. p_R + 5n^(-c-1) under the stated bounds on N, s, e; hence the switching clauses of `cor:multirow-size-switching` (size n^c, c < 2-eps, tails 2^w <= n^(2-c)/1024, no other hypothesis), `cor:crowded-size-switching`, `thm:few-light-rows-switching` and the combined theorem are restored for role-separated readers under column-sparseness, with light range up to width log2 n - O(log N) | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded obstructions checked, tool run, standing reviewer); the composition that `obs:composition-gap` found missing, on one probability space (Phi_0) for one tree (the recorded complete-term tree), with the recorded round bounds as its only input; supersedes `thm:pair-space-composition` and makes `lem:pair-space-round-bound` unnecessary; superseded 2026-09-18: `thm:current-term-composition` removes column-sparseness | [Theorem G and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-theorem), [scope](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-remaining) |
| `obs:dense-cube-obstruction` | Readers above size n^(5/3) whose pins fill a tail pattern cube of width in the light range are not column-sparse: the own-row rule leaves no valid code label for that pattern, and Lemma R fails; such readers' trees are short for other reasons (a consistently matched tail row satisfies a term), so a different argument is needed; two-role readers also remain, a two-role row at a code label changing the status of its own pins in the code | Obstruction; two-role readers removed from the obstruction 2026-09-18 (`thm:two-role-compact-composition`); closed 2026-09-18 by `thm:current-term-composition`: the all-pairs reader is covered and the checker decodes its moves | [Section 5](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#compact-composition-remaining) |
| `thm:two-role-compact-composition` | Two-role form of `thm:compact-pair-space-composition`: with code labels outside the moved row's own pinned labels and outside its filling row's pins, the decoder simulates the compact complete-term tree for two-role readers (the current term's alive pinned row is assigned by its round, so it is never moved later); for sparsely pinned, column-sparse readers with \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2 and tails 2^(w+6) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), any recorded two-role round bound p_R gives switching within the height budget except with probability p_R + 5 n^(-c-1); hence `thm:two-role-few-rows-switching`, `thm:two-role-exposed-switching` and `cor:two-role-size-switching` regain their switching clauses | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded two-role counterexample decoded by the tool, standing reviewer); richness with both exclusions costs n 2^(-e/29) + 2Mr exp(-s); superseded 2026-09-18: `thm:current-term-composition` removes column-sparseness | [Decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-decoder), [richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-richness), [theorem and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#two-role-compact-theorem) |
| `lem:current-term-decoder` | The pair-space code of the compact complete-term tree with the current-term rule (the code label of the k-th move lies in E(rho) n C(p) outside the pin labels of the current terms of the nodes up to and including the move's node; two-role: also outside the moved row's own pinned labels) is injective: a term carrying a pin (f_k, y_k) of an undecoded slot that is not falsified is pin-matched, so it is the current term, which the rule excludes, or comes after it; once the k-th move is decoded the decoder assigns the filling row f_k its label y_k = rho*(j_k) | Working lemma; exhaustive round trips (batches 7, 7b, 7c, 7d: every recorded configuration, the all-pairs reader), controls fail as predicted (no slot revelation: 9 failures; current-term labels allowed: 175 and 181 failures, every one through such a label) | [Decoder](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-decoder), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-tool) |
| `thm:current-term-composition` | Composition on Phi_0 without column-sparseness: with the current-term rule, richness is the count \|E(rho) n C(p)\| >= 3s for every tail pattern (failure Mr exp(-s)), and any recorded round bound p_R on the compact complete-term tree gives switching within the height budget except with probability p_R + 3n^(-c-1), for tails 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(16 r N^(3/2)(N+1)), s >= nu_0, N >= 8(c+1) log2 n + (2r+1)(16 + 4 log2 N), s >= (2c+1) ln n + ln r, with no hypothesis on the pins of a role-separated reader (two-role readers: sparse pinning on their light rows and \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2); hence the switching clauses of `cor:multirow-size-switching`, `cor:crowded-size-switching`, `thm:few-light-rows-switching`, `thm:multirow-mass-switching`, `thm:two-role-few-rows-switching`, `thm:two-role-exposed-switching` and `cor:two-role-size-switching` hold in the pair-space light range without column-sparseness or width conditions | Working theorem with full proof, passed through the verification gate (dependencies re-read, recorded obstructions checked, exhaustive checker, standing reviewer); supersedes `thm:compact-pair-space-composition`, `thm:two-role-compact-composition` and `lem:own-row-richness`; closes `obs:dense-cube-obstruction` and `obs:dense-pinning-obstruction`; extended conditionally to wide tail rows by `thm:wide-move-composition` (2026-09-18); the wide-row case closed for role-separated readers with split rows below size n^(2-2 eps) by `cor:split-rows-below-n2` (2026-09-18) | [Observation](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-observation), [richness](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-richness), [theorem and corollary](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-theorem), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#current-term-rule-remaining) |
| `thm:wide-move-composition` | Composition with wide tail rows (more than w literals, up to l): a wide row moved on the encoded path stays residual in rho* (kind and position in beta, answer in delta), the decoder of `lem:current-term-decoder` simulates the complete-term tree unchanged, and the count becomes (nu_1+1) A_w^(s-nu_1) (4rN)^nu_1 (2N(N+2))^nu_0 with A_w = 4rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1); for e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1)) and 2^(w+4) s <= e, if every path has at most nu_0 - 1 rounds and at most nu_1 = floor(N/48) wide moves except with probability p_R + p_W, the reader switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + p_W + (7/5)(Mr exp(-s) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+2)(2 + 3 log2 N))) | Conditional result: the wide-move bound p_W is proved for no reader, and `obs:shared-wide-cube` shows it fails on the complete-term tree; exhaustive round trips with unplaced moves (batch 8, 14 runs, 0 failures); superseded 2026-09-18 by `thm:wide-round-composition`, which needs no wide-move bound | [Composition](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-composition), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-remaining), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-tool) |
| `obs:shared-wide-cube` | For the pin-free reader of the n/2 terms [j in p] with one pattern p of l-1 literals, the complete-term tree has a path of length min(\|R' n B\|, N-1) whenever the flat contains exactly one label of C(p) and no row of B is matched into C(p), an event of probability at least N/(4n) together with \|R' n B\| >= N/8; a hole query at that label gives height one, so the complete-term tree, not the reader, fails per reader at the dichotomy's level n^(c-2), and wide rows need hole queries (a wide round by a smaller-side rule) with a wide-round bound; skipping terms with a residual wide row whose cube misses the flat is also necessary (the reader with distinct near-full patterns queries every residual row) | Obstruction with proof; finite check over every rho' in Phi_0 for every flat of F_2^3 of dimension 1 and 2 (tree heights recorded); resolved 2026-09-18 by the wide-round tree, whose cover is the cube's holes in the flat (`lem:wide-cover-bound`, at most two hole queries); the reader F_p switches at every size by `thm:single-row-wide-readers` (2026-09-18) | [Lemma and examples](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-obstruction), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rows-tool) |
| `lem:wide-annihilation` | On the wide-round tree a term whose residual wide row j (pattern p) is left unassigned while every hole of C(p) n Q is resolved is annihilated: the image of [j in p] is the sum of X_jx over x in C(p) n Q, and each summand times the leaf factor is a column exclusion or X_jx u_x(pi_0) of the hole-query nodes lemma; a cube missing Q gives the zero image, so the static skip is built in, and the q-skip refutation does not apply because only unplaced rows are skipped | Working proof; holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18) | [Lemma W0](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tree) |
| `lem:wide-cover-bound` | Wide-round tree: with a canonical minimum vertex cover C of the wide graph G (residual wide rows against the holes of Q in their pattern cubes, all terms) fixed at the root, wide rows of C queried as pigeons and the others by hole queries at their cube's unresolved holes, every path has at most \|C\| = nu(G) wide queries (Konig), each with at most N+2 answers; deterministic, in place of a per-node smaller-side rule, which the adaptive-rule counterexample forbids | Working proof; exhaustive census over every rho' in Phi_0 for every flat of F_2^3 of dimension 1 and 2 on three readers, wide queries never above the cover; holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18) | [Lemma W1](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-cover), [tool](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tool) |
| `lem:wide-matching-bound` | Pr[nu(G) >= k + t - 1] <= (\|Heavy\| N/n)^(d+1) + (e D N (N+1)/(t(n+1)))^t with d = ceil(log2 k), Heavy the holes of wide degree above D, the wide degree of x counting the wide incidences whose cube contains x; polynomial form: wide mass at most n^(2-2 eps)/(N+1), D = n^(1-eps)/(N+1), d = ceil(c'/eps)+2, t = ceil(c'/eps)+1 give Pr[nu(G) >= 2^d + t - 1] <= n^(-c') for polylogarithmic N and large n; at most n^c terms with wide patterns of at least w_1 literals, 2^(w_1) >= r(N+1) n^(c-1+2 eps), satisfy the mass condition | Working proof (rank lemma in its independent-points form and a union bound over residual rows); holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18); restated by rows in `lem:wide-matching-rows` (2026-09-18) | [Lemma W2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-matching) |
| `lem:wide-rounds-transfer` | Lemma 1 of the complete-term rule (trigger pairs of rounds pairwise disjoint, host not falsified by rho' and without a matched pair) holds on the wide-round tree, so by Lemma 2 of that rule the multi-row theorem and its size corollary (fewer than N/48 rounds except with probability 3n^(-c-1) for role-separated readers of size n^c, c < 2 - eps) hold on it, confirmed by re-reading steps (H1) to (H4); the few-light-rows, combined and two-role bounds are not transferred (a host can leave the consistent family through a wide row left uncovered) | Working proof for the multi-row bound; the exposed-row bounds remain to be transferred; holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18); the exposed-row bounds transferred on the reordered tree by `thm:round-bounds-on-wide-tree` | [Lemma W3](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-rounds), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-remaining) |
| `thm:wide-round-composition` | Composition on the wide-round tree with the current-term rule: wide rows never placed, one bit per node and the wide answers in delta, the decoder recomputing the cover from rho*; count 2 A_w^s (2N(N+2))^(nu_0) (2(N+2))^(nu_1) with A_w = 4rN(N+1)e/(n-N-e+1), s = floor(N/12) - 2r - nu_1, nu_0 = nu_1 = floor(N/48), N >= 48(2r+1), 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1)); if every path has at most nu_0 - 1 rounds except with probability p_R and nu(G) <= nu_1 except with probability p_W, the reader switches within the height budget except with probability p_R + p_W + (7/5)(Mr exp(-s) + 2^(-N/8 + (2r+2)(2 + 3 log2 N))); supersedes `thm:wide-move-composition` | Working proof (conditional on the two bounds for this tree); exhaustive round trips on the wide tree (batch 9, 14 runs, 0 failures, two controls failing as predicted); holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18) | [Theorem W](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-composition), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-tool) |
| `cor:pin-free-split-rows` | A pin-free reader with at most n^c terms whose rows split into light rows (patterns of at most w literals, 2^(w+4) s <= e <= n/(32 r N^4 (N+1))) and wide rows of wide mass at most n^(2-2 eps)/(N+1) (every wide pattern of at least w_1 literals with 2^(w_1) >= r(N+1) n^(c-1+2 eps)), \|B\| <= (n-2N-2)/2, switches under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 within the height budget except with probability 4n^(-c-1), for N polylogarithmic as stated and n large; the light and wide ranges overlap for c < 2 - 2 eps, so then every width is covered per row; mixed rows are absorbed up to n^(1-2 eps)/(2(N+1)) narrow incidences | Working proof (no rounds, so no round bound); holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18) | [Corollary 1](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-corollaries) |
| `cor:split-rows-below-n2` | Every role-separated reader of size n^c, 1 <= c < 2 - eps, whose tail rows split as in `cor:pin-free-split-rows`, switches under a uniform rho' in Phi_0 within the height budget except with probability 7n^(-c-1), under the multi-row theorem's conditions on N and 800e(N+1) <= n^(2-eps-c); for c < 2 - 2 eps every width is covered per row, closing the window case of wide rows for role-separated readers with split rows at sizes below n^(2-2 eps) | Working proof (multi-row round bound transferred by `lem:wide-rounds-transfer`, wide bound by `lem:wide-matching-bound`, composition by `thm:wide-round-composition`); holds for the reordered tree (`lem:wide-tree-reordered`, 2026-09-18) | [Corollary 2](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-corollaries), [remaining](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-remaining) |
| `lem:wide-tree-reordered` | The wide-round tree with its node reordered (the current term's wide rows first, then the round only if the term is still alive with an alive pin, then the light moves) keeps Lemmas W0 to W5, Theorem W and both corollaries of the wide-rounds entry with the same proofs, and coincides with the complete-term tree on readers without wide rows; from 2026-09-18 on T_w denotes the reordered tree | Working proof; exhaustive round trips on the reordered tree (batch 10, 14 runs, 0 failures, two controls failing as predicted) | [Lemma R0](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-tree), [checks](https://kbr.is-a.dev/math-research/#wide-rounds-exposed-tool) |
